**This is the ONLY open-work list** (owner, 2026-09-12). Handoff memories, the
audit-bus ledger and agent reports are evidence, not backlogs: anything still
open from them gets a line here, with the check that guards it once one exists.

**Layout (reconciled 2026-09-23, Q16):** (1) the live QUEUE, verbatim, first;
(2) every item still open from older sections, one line each with its current
status and a pointer into the archive; (3) the 2026-09-02 ledger's leftovers
(docs/audit/OPEN_ITEMS.md was retired, then deleted 2026-09-27; history in docs/archive/OPEN_ITEMS-2026-09-02.md); (4) the audit bus, by
reference. History (closed sections, narratives) is verbatim in
[docs/archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md](archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md),
with a reconcile log of every item closed and its evidence. Nothing was deleted.

**Done items leave this file (Q16, 2026-09-26):** a ticked `- [x]` item is moved
verbatim to the dated archive docs/archive/OPEN-done-YYYY-MM.md by
`npm run inventories:refresh` (scripts/archive-done.mjs); check:generated fails
while one is left here. The queue score, next free number and the done-item
guard check read this file AND the archives, so archiving never changes a count.

**`[~]` items say when they are done (2026-09-27):** put what is left as one or
more markers on the item, written out in full and ANDed together:
``done-when: sql `SELECT <one column>` => <expected>`` (read-only, first row),
`done-when: test <path>`, `done-when: issue #N closed`, or `done-when: pr #N merged`.
Only add markers when they cover everything that is left (a screenshot or a
review still to do means no marker yet). `node scripts/open-done-when.mjs` runs them;
the nightly `open-done-when` workflow opens one nightly-red issue listing the items
that are ready to tick. src/test/openPartlyDoneItemsSayDoneWhen.test.ts ratchets the
count of `[~]` items with no marker.

**Priority among the open queue items (lead's order, 2026-09-23 — money and
security first, then alerting, then the audit, then hygiene):** Q53 (DB outage
root cause), Q50 (card holds on cancelled jobs, Stripe side), Q3 (Stripe test
balance + monitor), Q30 (missed daily crons never catch up), Q2 (test runs raise
real alerts), Q42 (open alert-ledger items), Q9 (skipped money/authz review),
Q14 (Supabase security advisors), Q45 (prove a restore), Q58 (one place
for everything open), Q52 (false-green hunt), Q54 (front/back parity), Q35 (exhaustive
audit), Q44 (partial gates turn main red), Q11/Q15/Q39 (Sentry + error
screens), Q10 (owner-side), then the rest in number order. An item's own line
is the source of truth for its state; this sentence only orders them.

- [ ] **Q7 WebKit only: the bottom nav isn't frosted. OWNER (device), re-checked 2026-09-26: still unmeasured in WebKit.** Nothing code-side can settle it. The dock is signed-in only (guest /browse renders no MobileNav, checked at 375), the cloud session that took this item had no test-account credentials, and Playwright WebKit on Linux is not the iOS compositor, so no CI run can show WKWebView frost either. What the source does (MobileNav.tsx:705-745): the full-width curtain carries `backdropFilter`/`WebkitBackdropFilter: blur(32px) saturate(170%)` together with `maskImage`/`WebkitMaskImage` on the SAME element, and the pill carries `blur(40px) saturate(180%)` inside the nav, whose own style has `transform` + `willChange: transform` (the hide-on-scroll slide). Both are inline styles, so the CSS minifier cannot have dropped the -webkit- form. **Ask:** on the iOS 26.1 simulator or a device, signed in, open /home, scroll the feed under the dock and send one screenshot. **Recommendation:** if the pill is frosted and only the band above it is clear, the curtain's mask is the suspect (test by removing `WebkitMaskImage` alone). If the pill is clear too, test with `willChange` removed from the nav. Fix whichever one screenshot proves, and add a pixel-variance check under the dock to a WebKit spec.

- [ ] **Q743 Queued prod runs are silently cancelled: 9 workflows share one concurrency group (found 2026-09-27, lead).** a11y-webkit-prod, e2e-abuse-notifications, e2e-journeys, e2e-real-backend, loading-states-refresh, privacy-journey, press-every-control, prod-audit and slow-network all hold job-level `concurrency: prod-lifecycle-shared-accounts` (cancel-in-progress false). GitHub keeps ONE pending run per group, so a second queued run cancels the first: loading-states run 36294688957 (dispatch 04:34:12Z) was cancelled after 8s when press 36294859009 queued behind a11y 36294688001; loading-states also shows cancelled on 36272891811 and 36212148356. The reporter then counts it as no result, so a red hides. Fix: a real FIFO lock (e.g. a wait-for-older-runs step or a DB advisory lease) instead of the group, or a watchdog that re-dispatches any run cancelled while pending. Guard: a test over .github/workflows that no two prod workflows can queue into the same GitHub concurrency group, red on today's 9. **STATUS 2026-09-27 (fix landed, live proof pending):** the group stays as the mutual exclusion; in front of it every locked job (12 jobs in the 9 workflows) now `needs:` a lock-free `wait-accounts` job running `scripts/e2e/wait-shared-accounts.mjs`, which polls the Actions API and joins only when no locked job anywhere is waiting in the group and no older schedule/dispatch run is still on its way to it (FIFO by created_at). A run parked at workflow level (prod-load) is not waited for, so no deadlock; a waiter that times out (350 min) skips the locked job and the notify job reports red, never a silent cancel. More victims measured: e2e-journeys 36215839785, 36215921402, 36217255758 (2026-09-26 dispatch storm) and press 36297157445 (cancelled 05:26:28Z two seconds after loading-states 36297208529 was dispatched behind prod-audit 36290267474). Dry-run against the live API (while loading-states 36297208529 held the lock) answered `wait: Press every control 36297439015 is waiting for the lock` (correct). GUARD: src/test/sharedAccountLockQueues.test.ts, red on main before this fix (12 violations, one per locked job) plus `decide()` cases on the measured shapes; prodWorkflowSpacing rule 5 counts the waiter as 0 min (it only waits for dispatches, which the model never counted). Tick when a run shows the waiter holding a second entrant back instead of a cancel.

5. **Push notifications (Q82): three things only you can do.** The code
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "cut it when main is current".** Claude runs fastlane beta from main
   once the open landings are in and main CI is green; you install it and open the app once.
   cause is fixed and pushed (see Q82), but no phone has the fix yet.
   (a) **Cut a TestFlight build from main** (at or after the Q82 fix commit)
   and install it on your iPhone. The only builds with the AppDelegate
   forwarding are ce4d38d59 (2026-09-02) and later, and every build so far
   also has the boot race (it depends on timing, so it may not hit every
   launch). (b) **On that build: sign in, go to Home, tap "Enable" on the
   notifications pill, then Allow.** The pill only shows after you've posted
   or applied, or on a later launch more than an hour after install. If you tapped "Don't
   Allow" on any earlier build, iOS will not ask again: turn it on in
   Settings > Helpr > Notifications first, then cold-launch the app. Within
   seconds `select count(*) from push_tokens` should be 1 and
   analytics_events should show `push_token_saved`. If it doesn't, the
   analytics events say which step failed. (c) **Only if you run the app
   straight from Xcode on your phone:** those builds get SANDBOX APNs tokens,
   and prod sends to the production APNs host (`APNS_USE_SANDBOX` is unset),
   so APNs rejects them with BadDeviceToken and the sender deletes the row.
   TestFlight/App Store builds are unaffected. Leave this alone unless you
   test from Xcode. APNs credentials are fine: APNS_KEY_ID, APNS_TEAM_ID
   (digest matches P85MCK558V), APNS_BUNDLE_ID (digest matches com.Helpr)
   and APNS_AUTH_KEY are all set in Supabase secrets. Whether the .p8 key
   itself is valid can't be checked without a real token to send to.

CHECKED 2026-09-26: fix on main, guard present; the item's own tick condition is the owner's local Mac hygiene log, which I cannot read from this worktree — remains open. Finished agents' worktrees pile up.** Every worktree-isolated agent
  leaves a LOCKED worktree under .claude/worktrees/, and prune-git-hygiene
  never touches locked ones (by design, since running agents lock theirs). Add a
  safe rule: a worktree whose agent has finished (its branch is merged into
  origin/main or has no commits, no process has cwd there, and it's older
  than 2h) is unlocked and removed; anything unmerged is reported, never deleted.
  DONE: rule in scripts/lib/worktreeHygiene.mjs, called by
  scripts/prune-git-hygiene.mjs. A LOCKED worktree under <main>/.claude/worktrees/
  is unlocked and removed (`git worktree remove`, never --force) when HEAD is in
  origin/main (rev-list 0, or every commit upstream by patch-id via `git cherry`),
  status is clean, no process cwd is inside (lsof; unknown = remove nothing), the
  pid in its lock reason (if any) is not running, and it is older than 2h. Unmerged
  or dirty ones are REPORTED in the hygiene output/log, never removed. Main is never
  a candidate; locks elsewhere are still skipped. GUARD:
  src/test/pruneAgentWorktrees.test.ts (fixture repo: bare origin + clone + 7 linked
  worktrees; plan AND apply; 11 @mutate, all killed, incl. restoring the old

What still auto-suspends with no human is `auto_restrict_repeat_violators`: on any violation type it does not exclude (it excludes admin_action, admin_warning, cancel_with_helper, off_platform, job_denial, no_show), it counts ALL of the user's violations ever (`COUNT(*)`, no type filter, no time window): 1 = final warning, 2 = 7-day temp_banned, 3 = 30 days. So an old off_platform warning plus one later harassment/low-rating row suspends a new account for 7 days, unreviewed. OWNER QUESTION (held for the pop-up): (a) close the warn-first half as already done, or (b) make `auto_restrict_repeat_violators` warn-first too (second trip within 7 days to suspend, and count only its own types). Recommendation (b); Claude builds it (migration + test) on your answer. [DONE: spelled-number evasion heuristic, 20260915020258 (Q266)]; [DONE 2026-09-27, owner answer (b): warn-first auto-restrict, migration 20260927043454_auto_restrict_warn_first. It counts only its own types (NOT IN the six excluded), only rows from the last 7 days: 1st trip = final warning (always notified), 2nd within 7 days = 7-day temp_banned + admin alert, 3rd within 7 days = 30 days (kept; reachable only if an admin lifts the 7-day ban early without reversing strikes), 4+ = admin alert. Layers read: trigger `auto_restrict_repeat_violators_tg` (AFTER INSERT on user_violations), the function (live proacl before `{postgres=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres}`, restated REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated), admin display AutoRestrictedRail (lifetime count, display only, unchanged), BanDialog writes (admin_* types, excluded), notifications (insert order kept for seedNeverNotifiesReal), legal ladder copy in CommunitySection (updated). Proof: PGlite src/test/pglite/autoRestrictWarnFirst.pglite.mjs = 6 FAILED on the old body, ALL PASS (11) with the migration applied 3x and with --tree. Guard: src/test/autoRestrictWarnFirst.test.ts (newest definition keeps NOT IN list + 7-day window + REVOKE; red 1/4 with the window removed, 3/4 with the migration removed).]; still open: `cash` over-fire, ladder retune after 2-3 weeks of real data, fraud signals beyond burst-job.

7. **Turn off Supabase's GitHub auto-deploy to production (Q121). Top priority.**
   On every push to main, Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations
   (and deploys edge functions) to prod within about a minute. Our deploy
   workflow's safety checks (lint, replay test, destructive-DDL stop) run after
   that, so for the last 24h (24 migrations) they have not blocked anything.
   Measured from prod's own logs 2026-09-23 (Q117). What you do: Supabase
   dashboard > Project Settings > Integrations > GitHub, and switch off
   automatic deploys to the production branch (exact toggle name not verified).
   Until then, each new migration turns db-deploy red by design (the new
   provenance check), so the side channel stays visible.
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner asked "whats the best option?"): switch it OFF.** Measured: db-deploy
   36261725456 (18:16Z) judged 124 prod versions since the cutoff, 108 with a db-deploy receipt and 16
   acknowledged side-channel applies, the newest of them 2026-09-23 ~16:10Z (Q194). So nothing has come
   in by the side channel for about 3 days, but that is not proof the toggle is off. Off is the only
   setting where db-deploy's lint, replay and destructive-DDL checks actually run before prod changes.
   Still the owner's dashboard click; the provenance check stays as the detector either way.

7. **Backups (Q45): the restore is proven weekly now; five things are yours.**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-23 (owner pop-ups): (a) yes, Pro is paid and intended; (b) keep 14-day retention; (c) back up uploaded files EXCEPT id-documents, encrypted (work item Q147); (d) no PITR (no paid upgrade); (e) BACKUP_PASSPHRASE and ban_fingerprint_salt go in the owner's password manager (owner to-do).**
   Measured 2026-09-23. Nothing below is needed for the drill to stay green.
   (a) **Is the Supabase org really on Pro?** The API says `plan: "pro"`, and
   `supabase backups list` shows 7 daily platform backups. Every doc said
   "free tier". If you are paying $25/mo, you already have in-place daily
   restores for 7 days. If you did not mean to be on Pro, that is a bill.
   (b) **Backup retention is 14 days, not 90.** The repo caps artifacts and
   logs at 14 days (`maximum_allowed_days` 90). Raising it to 90 is one
   setting, but it lengthens EVERY workflow's artifacts, and on the free
   GitHub plan that can go past the artifact-storage quota (billed). The
   alternative is a monthly copy somewhere else. Your call.
   (c) **Storage files (photos, ID documents) are in NO backup**: 152 objects,
   about 10 MB (proof-photos 64, job-photos 61, message-attachments 18,
   user-documents 4, avatars 4, id-documents 1). The platform backup excludes
   them too. Backing them up needs a service-role key as an Actions secret and
   puts ID documents (encrypted) into a GitHub artifact: a privacy trade-off.
   (d) **PITR** (restore to the minute) is a paid add-on, about $100/mo plus
   Small compute. Without it, worst-case loss is about 24 h (platform) or
   24 h + GitHub's up-to-7 h schedule delay (ours). You said no paid upgrades,
   so this is only the measured gap.
   (e) **Two secrets that no backup holds.** `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`: is there a
   copy outside GitHub? If not, losing the GitHub account also loses every
   backup. `ban_fingerprint_salt` (vault): it cannot be re-created, and
   without it every stored ban fingerprint stops matching after a restore.
   Want it (encrypted) in the backup, or kept in your password manager?

   deleting them at once may be wrong too. Options: (a) keep them 4 years after
   deletion, then delete automatically; (b) delete them with the account; (c) keep
   forever. I recommend (a), but it is a legal call. No W-9s exist in prod yet.

    Measured (Chromium, poster-e2e, settlePage): return visits 0 @375 / 0 @1440 in 8 of 8 loads; a device's FIRST visit
    still shifts once, 0.0589 @375 / 0.0216 @1440 (nothing seen yet to hold), now listed in page-settle.spec KNOWN.
    Correction: the earlier "prod 0.0589 → fixed 0" for the below-rows build was not re-confirmed on prod (prod
    still served the bar on top when re-measured). WebKit shots ~/.lh-shots/mq28-top-{375,1440}.png (reviewed ok).**

29. **PR #1810 (Q392): which parish job-match alert design stays? (2026-09-26).** PR #1810 and main
    (20260926041132, Q225/V-008) both fix "parish alert arrives before early access shows the job", differently:
    (A) KEEP MAIN'S queue-only design (every send goes through the 1-minute sweep, so elite/no-delay users hear up
    to ~60s later; nothing sent inside the funding write) and ADD the PR's block check (`user_blocks`, both ways)
    and shared `job_announceable_to()` gate. A user who deletes the alert can be told again if the job reopens.
    (B) TAKE THE PR'S design: elite/no-delay users told inline at funding (incl. email via net.http_post inside the
    escrow write), a permanent per-(user, job) `job_match_queue` ledger so nobody is ever told twice, block check;
    main's `parish_match_alert_queue` and its sweep step are retired.
    Measured live (pg_get_functiondef, prod): neither `notify_helpers_on_job_post` nor `deliver_parish_match_alert`
    checks `user_blocks` today; `deliver_job_match`/`job_announceable_to` do not exist on prod.
    Not blocked by this: the PR's instant-match half, digest gating, export_my_data line, and Q401 (auth-page
    noindex) are being landed separately. The PR's old-timestamp migrations must never reach main as-is.
    **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "A + never twice": keep main's queue-only send path, add the
    `user_blocks` check (both ways) and `job_announceable_to()` gate, AND write parish sends into the permanent
    per-(user, job) ledger so a deleted alert is never re-sent when the job reopens. Lands after the instant half.**
    **DONE 2026-09-26: landed as migration 20260926195608 (Q392 parish half).**
30. **press-every-control: press one representative per repeated list row? (#1582, 2026-09-26).** Run
    36230166945: /admin?view=jobs alone took 97 min (412 presses), mostly the same four controls on every job
    card. Landed without asking: shards now claim rows from one shared queue (Q725), so a heavy row no longer
    strands the rest of its shard. Still yours: (A) keep pressing every card (complete, slow; ~97 min a run on
    that view), or (B) press every control on the first N cards of a repeated list and only count the rest
    (much faster; a defect that shows only on card N+1 would be missed). Default until answered: (A).
31. **Group crews: three money rules (Q728/Q729, 2026-09-27).** Group jobs are off
    (GROUP_JOBS_ENABLED=false), so nothing is charged under these yet. (A) CREW_FEE_PAYS_UNCONFIRMED:
    a late-cancel fee is shared by a member the poster OFFERED the job to but who never confirmed.
    Built as yes (your Q407(13) answer); confirm. (B) CREW_COMPLETES_WHEN_HIRED_DONE: an under-filled
    crew completes when every hired member is done, and the unfilled shares go back to the poster.
    Built as yes; confirm. (C) New: when a crew dispute or an unfilled slot returns a member's share,
    only the share itself (budget + urgent fee) goes back. The platform fee, sales tax and any gift
    card on that charge are NOT returned. Should they be returned in proportion to the share?
    ANSWERED 2026-09-27: yes, pro-rata. BUILT (see the ANSWERED block above); guard src/test/edge/process-scheduled-payouts-crew.test.ts.

The Stripe TEST balance can now cover them (GET /v1/balance, livemode false: $1,640.69 available, $673.21 pending). RECOMMENDATION: a weekly `process-scheduled-payouts?include_seed=1` run in TEST mode only (gated on E2E_STRIPE_MODE == test, so it can never run after stripe-sandbox-off.sh), which proves the transfer leg on real Stripe; since Q93 its seed failures go to the digest and never page. Say yes and a lane builds it (money path: lh-money-escrow review). (b) UNCHANGED: fixtures 5eed0a10/0a20-...-10 (released), -11 (payout_pending), -12 (disputed) have no Stripe session/PI and -12 has 0 disputes rows. RECOMMENDATION: delete these six (tips, reviews and messages cascade) and stop prod-seed claiming money states it cannot back; they only feed digest-only seed findings. (c) NOT REPRODUCIBLE LIVE: 0 group jobs on prod have disputed_at set. Code read (live pg_get_functiondef): rpc_withdraw_dispute and the settle functions set payout_pending and never clear disputed_at, and process-scheduled-payouts pays only `disputed_at IS NULL`, so a withdrawn dispute relies on auto-release phase 2, which excludes group jobs. Needs a PGlite reproduction before any fix. Was: **Q92 Seed money rows no path will ever settle (found by Q90).** (a)
  24 seed jobs sit in payout_pending with payout_scheduled_at still in the
  future (2026-09-23 08:30Z); both payout crons skip is_seed, so each becomes
  a seed payout_pending_stranded tomorrow, and the nightly money journeys

  lead: a GROUP job whose dispute was withdrawn or auto-resolved keeps

  skips it, and auto-release Phase 2 excludes group jobs. If both hold, its
  payout never runs. Reproduce before fixing.

- [~] **Q295 /admin?view=credentials "Open" scored "no observable change (focus moved, nothing else)" (press run 35837735324, found by Q128).** **STATUS 2026-09-26 (cloud/open-ops): FIXED in code, live row not re-measured.** Root cause by code read: SignedOpenLink awaited createSignedUrl and only then called window.open(url, '_blank', 'noopener'). WebKit (Safari and the iOS WKWebView) drops the click's user activation across the await and blocks the popup, and with noopener window.open returns null, so nothing opened and nothing was said. Now src/lib/openSignedDocument.ts opens the tab inside the click, nulls its opener, points it at the signed URL, closes it with a toast if signing fails, and toasts a refused popup; signing errors are reported. Guard: src/lib/openSignedDocument.test.ts (5 @mutate, each red). NOT measured: whether Chromium's press run saw the old popup at all (the item's other hypothesis, a popup after the after-snapshot); the press run's credentials row is the live proof. SignedOpenLink (AdminCredentialQueue.tsx) awaits createSignedUrl and then `window.open(…, "_blank", "noopener")`; the press listens for new pages on the context, so either the popup landed after the after-snapshot, the click lost user activation across the await, or the signed URL failed without a toast. Not measured (needs a live admin session). Guard to name when fixed: the press run's credentials row green, or a unit test on SignedOpenLink's outcome.

- [~] **Q349 One real account's avatar object is stored `Cache-Control: no-cache` and nothing I read wrote it (2026-09-24, Q329 remainder).** storage.objects avatars/7f65ef12-…/avatar.jpg (real, non-seed, not the owner; signed up 2026-05-03), written 2026-09-20 21:52Z with owner_id NULL (service-role). complete-signup is the only edge uploader and runs at signup, not months later; scripts/audit/prod-seed.mjs touches only the helper test account. Find the writer (storage API logs for that minute), then decide with the owner whether to rewrite the object's metadata. Not touched: real user data. **2026-09-26 (cloud/open-perf-client): code side DONE, OWNER TO-DO below.** Found how an object gets `no-cache`: Storage stores a raw HTTP upload that sends no cache-control header as `no-cache`. storage-js never does that (it always sends `max-age=3600`), so complete-signup (storage-js, no cacheControl option, so max-age=3600) is not the writer. The repo had 5 raw uploads, 4 without the header, one sending the invalid "3600" (scripts/audit/prod-seed.mjs ×2, the message-attachments probe, the prod-audit harness, the privacy spec). None writes to `avatars` for a real user, so the Q349 writer is still NOT in this repo: an ad-hoc service-role call (owner_id NULL) on 2026-09-20 21:52Z. All 5 now send `cache-control: max-age=3600`. GUARD: src/test/rawStorageUploadsSetCacheControl.test.ts (every `x-upsert` header set also sends max-age; red on the old files with 5 sites listed, 2/2 @mutate killed). Not done from here: this container has no service role and the Supabase MCP failed to connect, so the storage logs and the object's metadata could not be read. **OWNER TO-DO (5 min):** Supabase Dashboard → Logs → Storage, 2026-09-20 21:50–21:55 UTC, path `avatars/7f65ef12-…/avatar.jpg`, and note the user agent / IP of the POST. **Recommendation:** leave the object as it is. It is one real person's photo, costs one extra download per view, and rewriting it means re-uploading that person's data with the service role. Close this once the writer is named; if the writer turns out to be a tool we still use, give it the same header.

- [ ] **Q444 A group job can be marked released while a roster member is unpaid (lh-money-escrow review of Q355 part 2, 2026-09-26, code read only, not reproduced).** supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/transferCreated.ts flips jobs.payment_status to 'released' for ANY transfer that has a payout_transfers row, whenever the job is payout_pending/escrow/released, with no roster check. process-scheduled-payouts deliberately keeps a group job payout_pending until every roster member has a pending/paid payout_transfers row (the allRosterPaid block). So when one member's transfer throws ('Scheduled payout failed'), another member's transfer.created webhook can release the job; the cron selects payment_status = 'payout_pending' and never retries the unpaid member. Q355 part 2's money-held rule counts that state as still owed, so the alert stays open, but the payout itself is stuck. Fix: give transferCreated the same roster test (only the transfer that completes the roster may flip a group job), with a PGlite/edge test red on the current handler. Needs a money-escrow review.

Reads as a race that closes the placeholder thread when the network is slow. The same deep link opens the thread with a reply box for helpr-e2e-poster-0902 on prod at normal speed (checked 2026-09-24). Evidence: the run's artifacts and trace, copied to the session scratchpad. ROOT CAUSE (trace): not a race. The inbox load, a full confirming refetch and then the placeholder lookup ran in series; a just-hired thread has no messages, so the refetch can never return it, and the placeholder's jobs read was still pending at the 30s timeout. FIXED 2026-09-24: the placeholder is built alongside the refetch (src/pages/messages/useMessagesData.ts). GUARD: src/pages/messages/useMessagesData.test.tsx "builds the placeholder while the confirming refetch is still in flight" (@mutate proven). PENDING: an e2e-journeys run green on the slow profile closes #1719.
- [~] **Q383** (LOW, money, found during ME-006 2026-09-25, code read only): STATUS 2026-09-26 (cloud/open-security-money, c71f006e1): MEASURED in Stripe test mode, the account's default payment-method configuration offers affirm, afterpay_clearpay, klarna, cashapp, us_bank_account, amazon_pay and more beside card. FIXED: the tip Checkout Session now sets payment_method_types TIP_PAYMENT_METHOD_TYPES = [card, link] (_shared/tipFees.ts; Apple/Google Pay are card wallets and stay); a test-mode session of exactly that shape was created and expired. auto-tip-charge already charges only a saved card. GUARD src/test/edge/create-payment.test.ts (tip session asserts [card, link]; @mutate red). OWNER QUESTION (the refund rule): should a tip ever be refunded? Recommendation: tips are final in the app (no code path refunds one today); an admin Dashboard refund of a tip must use reverse_transfer=true and refund_application_fee=true so the Helpr's share and the fee come back together, and we write that into the admin runbook. Original text: the tip fee covers Stripe's CARD rate (2.9% + 30c). Tip Checkout sessions set no `payment_method_types`, so any other method the account enables (the stripeFees.ts comment lists Klarna/Affirm/Afterpay at 5.99% + 30c) costs more than the fee collected and the platform pays the difference; the Helpr still gets the full tip. Also: no code path refunds a tip, and a Dashboard refund of a tip does not reverse the Helpr's transfer unless `reverse_transfer` is set. Decide: restrict tip sessions to `card`, and write the tip refund rule down.

- [ ] **Q390** (MEDIUM, OA-004, 2026-09-25): OWNER QUESTION (2026-09-26): move the native session mirror from @capacitor/preferences (NSUserDefaults plist, in device backups) to the iOS Keychain / Android Keystore (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly)? Recommendation: yes, before launch, in a native release; it needs a new plugin in the .ipa/.apk, so it ships only with a build, and a real-device check (relaunch, Offload App, WebKit localStorage eviction) that cannot run in a cloud session. The misleading `keychainStorageAdapter` name is fixed (now `preferencesStorageAdapter`; guard `src/test/secureStorageNamesAreTrue.test.ts`). Open: owner decision whether to move the native mirror to a Keychain-backed plugin (kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly); needs a device check that sessions still survive relaunch, Offload App and WebKit localStorage eviction. No guard yet for the storage choice itself.

- [~] **Q393** (group jobs, found by the group-jobs lane 2026-09-25; reproduced on prod in DO blocks that rolled back, on the one seed group job def709bf, is_seed, unpaid): a Helpr who leaves or is removed from a crew stays on the job. D1 the poster removes the lead from a staffing crew: jobs.helper_id still names them (address, messaging, party status) and their application stays accepted. D2 crew member #2 cannot leave (helper_cancel_booking not_authorized); the lead who leaves keeps their roster row (still paid by the roster payout fan-out) and the stale row blocks a replacement hire (roster_full). D3 the crew tracker's Working step reads the JOB's before photo, which members 2..N never write. The first fix (commits e28bf61a1/f85434722) was taken out of PR #1790 after the lh-money-escrow review; REWORKED 2026-09-25 on branch group-fix, not deployed, all six review findings addressed: migration supabase/migrations/20260925140148_group_roster_departure.sql: (1) HIGH, trigger trg_sync_job_after_roster_departure clears the departed lead under a transaction-local app.roster_departure flag, set around that one statement and restored straight after; enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock is restated from its newest text (20260915101102) with a carve-out for that flag, helper_id -> NULL only, and the only function that sets the flag is the trigger; (2) MEDIUM, on every lead change (poster removal, the lead leaving through helper_cancel_booking, account deletion) jobs.helper_confirmed_at / helper_dayof_confirmed_at become the NEW lead's own roster stamps (NULL with no lead) and response_deadline plus the three reminder sent-ats are cleared, so poster_cancel_job never prices a committed-tier fee for a lead who never confirmed; helper_cancel_booking changes the lead only through that trigger; (3) LOW, the crew tracker's Working step reads every member's own roster before photo, the lead's included, and the job-level photo clears nobody; (4) LOW, lock order documented in the migration header (Postgres locks the deleted roster row before any BEFORE ROW trigger, so the poster's DELETE cannot take jobs first; a simultaneous poster removal + self-leave of the same member ends in one 40P01 abort, nothing half-written, member off the crew either way); (5) LOW, create-payment cancel_escrow refuses (409 useCancelJob) any job with group_job_helpers rows, fail-closed 503 on a read error; (6) LOW, a group job's lead with no roster slot cancels through the single-helper path. GUARDS: src/test/groupJobRosterLifecycle.test.ts "leaving the crew leaves the job" (11 @mutate on the migration, 11/11 red, incl. the money-lock carve-out, the flag reset, the stamp follow, the legacy fallback and the photo rule; the flag's setter inventory must be exactly the trigger); src/test/edge/create-payment.test.ts (crew-hired refusal + fail-closed read, @mutate red); behavioural src/test/pglite/groupRosterDeparture.pglite.mjs runs every case through prod's full BEFORE UPDATE chain on jobs (award gate, cancellation-requires-rpc, status transition, helper whitelist, hire-columns lock, funding gate, poster money lock, field escalation, arrival integrity; newest migration text, cross-checked against effectiveDefs; helper_cancel_booking and the tracker gate md5 = prod 2026-09-25): R1-R4 red before, A1-A24 green after 3x apply; without the money-lock carve-out A1, A2, A4, A5, A21, A22 fail ("Posters may not modify jobs.helper_id once checkout has opened"); without the stamp follow A19-A22 fail; without the flag reset A23 fails. To verify on prod after db-deploy: `SELECT p.proname, md5(pg_get_functiondef(p.oid)), p.proacl FROM pg_proc p WHERE p.pronamespace='public'::regnamespace AND p.proname IN ('sync_job_after_roster_departure','enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock','helper_cancel_booking','enforce_job_tracking_arrival_gate');` and `SELECT tgname, pg_get_triggerdef(oid) FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgrelid='public.group_job_helpers'::regclass AND tgname='trg_sync_job_after_roster_departure';`, then re-run the D1-D3 rollback probes on def709bf. NEEDS a fresh REVIEW-ONLY lh-money-escrow + lh-authz-rls pass on the rework. **SUPERSEDED IN PART by Q407 (2026-09-25, same branch, commits after 19d40e3ca):** a crew has no lead, so the lead re-point, the stamp follow and the app.roster_departure carve-out are gone: migration 20260925154606 restates sync_job_after_roster_departure (rejects the departed Helpr's application, writes no jobs row) and enforce_poster_jobs_money_lock (its 20260915101102 text, no carve-out). - [~] **Q394** (OWNER QUESTION, group jobs, 2026-09-25): when the poster cancels a booked crew late, the cancellation fee goes to jobs.helper_id (the crew lead) only. Should it be split across the crew (evenly, like the roster payout fan-out), go to the lead, or go to nobody on a crew job? Not built; blocks group-jobs turn-on (VN-52). Guard to name when built: a PGlite proof of the fee path on a 3-member crew. **ANSWERED by Q407 (owner, 2026-09-25): split EVENLY across the hired crew. BUILT, not deployed:** poster_cancel_job writes one crew_cancellation_fee_shares row per hired member (fee = round(frozen share cents x ladder % / 100)); void-cancelled-payments pays each row with its own Stripe transfer and key `cancel-fee-<job>-<member>`, skips rows marked paid, repairs the ledger from the Stripe transfer group instead of paying twice, re-prices every row (F-MONEY-32) and retries pending/failed rows after the job settles (Part D). Whether a hired member who never confirmed counts is ONE rule, public.crew_fee_pays_unconfirmed() / CREW_FEE_PAYS_UNCONFIRMED (default true = every hired member; owner to confirm). GUARDS: src/test/pglite/groupCrewNoLead.pglite.mjs A7-A15, src/test/edge/void-cancelled-payments-crew.test.ts (7 @mutate, all red), src/test/groupCrewNoLead.test.ts 4b.

- [~] **Q397** (found by Q105(4), 2026-09-25): run_missed_cron_catch_up() (cron-missed-slot-catch-up, every 10 minutes) costs ~288 ms per call (pg_stat_statements 2026-09-25: 291 calls, mean 287.5 ms, 83.7 s total, as much as sweep_dead_crons) because its candidate query runs 5 subqueries correlated on jobid over cron.job_run_details (no usable index; supabase_admin owns the table) for every active job. FIXED 2026-09-25 (branch cloud/q397-cron-catchup-perf, draft PR, NOT YET LIVE: not merged, so db-deploy has not run): migration 20260925155322_catch_up_candidates_one_scan joins the due jobs (active, daily/weekly, slot past grace and inside its period) to cron.job_run_details ONCE and computes the 5 facts as aggregate FILTERs with the old bounds; the rest of the body is 20260923172145's. Two deliberate refinements: ORDER BY slot, jobid (two jobs on one slot were in plan order) and last_failure ties on start_time broken by runid DESC (only the quoted text can differ). PROOF: src/test/pglite/cronCatchUpOneScan.pglite.mjs (applied 3x; old body vs new body in the same transaction, identical returned jsonb, cron_catchup_runs, error_logs, schedules and commands run, with no policy rows and with every job safe: 37 named edge cases incl. never ran, ran late, a slot missed twice, disabled, changed schedule, all PASS; 150 seeded random histories x 60 jobs around every bound, 150/150 identical; MUTATE=1 (at-slot bound made inclusive) is RED: 4 FAIL, only 73/150 rounds identical); cronCatchUp.pglite.mjs now runs its 55 Q30/Q207 checks against the new body: ALL PASS. GUARDS: src/test/cronRunHistoryScannedOnce.test.ts (KNOWN_CORRELATED = cron_dispatch_health 1 only; red without the migration, 5 vs 0; red with the old entry left in, stale; its new @mutate killed); cronCatchUpPolicy.test.ts regexes made alias-agnostic and its 8 @mutate lines plus cronHttpRequestsAreTagged's 1 re-pointed at the new migration (all 10 killed). TO VERIFY LIVE after db-deploy: pg_get_functiondef('public.run_missed_cron_catch_up()'::regprocedure) contains 'hist AS' and no 'WHERE d.jobid = j.jobid'; pg_proc.proacl for it grants EXECUTE to service_role only; pg_stat_statements mean_exec_time for `SELECT public.run_missed_cron_catch_up()` on calls after the deploy (was 287.5 ms over 291 calls); cron_catchup_runs keeps getting the same kinds of rows.
- [~] **Q705** (LOW-MEDIUM, messaging authz, found by the lh-authz-rls review of the group no-lead redesign 2026-09-25, code read only, not checked live): `can_message_in_job` branch 4 ("the poster messaged THIS sender first", newest in 20260914201350_messaging_lockout_24h_after_completion.sql) has no membership or status check, so once a poster has messaged someone, that person can keep messaging on the job after being removed from a crew or having their application rejected, with no end. Fix: require the sender to still be a party (hired, pending applicant, or crew member via group_job_helpers) or within the post-completion 24h window; PGlite case for a removed crew member and a rejected applicant. **FIXED 2026-09-25 (branch msg-authz, not yet live), extended the same day by the owner's both-ways rule (Q407 addendum 14):** (1) 20260925175953_message_gate_poster_first_requires_current_party.sql restates can_message_in_job from its newest text (20260914201350; 20260919220233 changed only job_messaging_closes_at; 20260925154606_group_crew_has_no_lead.sql mentions it only in a comment) with branch 4 also requiring a live application (status pending or accepted). (2) 20260925230845_messaging_closes_both_ways_off_job.sql adds is_off_job(job, user), an internal predicate: the user applied or was offered the job and is no longer a party (not the poster, not hired, no pending offer, not on the crew, no live application). It restates can_message_in_job from (1) with branch 2 admitting an offeree only while direct_offer_status = 'pending' (settles Q420(a)), restates can_send_message_to_in_job from 20260914215014 with AND NOT is_off_job(job, receiver) (settles Q420(b): the poster can no longer message someone who is off the job), and adds get_off_job_thread_state(job, other), which the client reads. It returns 'self' when the caller is off the job, 'other' when the other person is and the caller already has a thread with them, else NULL. Grants: can_message_in_job and is_off_job are service_role only; can_send_message_to_in_job and get_off_job_thread_state are authenticated + service_role; every REVOKE names PUBLIC and anon. CLIENT (review M2): src/lib/offJobGate.ts. ChatView asks get_off_job_thread_state and ChatComposer replaces the composer with a read-only notice ("This conversation is closed because you're / they're no longer on this job. You can still read everything here.", not role-based) on both sides, ahead of the poster-first lock. sendHandlers turns a 42501 RLS refusal the server attributes to off-the-job into a non-retryable `refused` bubble, not the retryable "didn't go through" one. The stale ChatView comment on can_message_in_job's classes is rewritten. PGlite (`PGLITE_DIR=~/.lh-pglite-probe npx tsx src/test/pglite/messageGateCurrentParty.pglite.mjs`, 89 checks, each migration 3x): every function is read from its effective definition through effectiveDefs/blankSqlComments. The crew removal is a real DELETE firing the real trg_sync_job_after_roster_departure (20260925140148, restated by 20260925154606), and the real trg_group_job_has_no_lead refuses a lead. The harness exits 1 on a tree without 20260925140148. BEFORE, the removed crew member, the rejected applicant and declined/expired offerees can message the poster and be messaged by them. AFTER, all of those are refused both ways, while the hired Helpr, pending and accepted applicants, a pending offeree and a current crew member are allowed both ways. The 24h post-completion window and the cancelled-job close are unchanged. The poster-first lock and the stranger refusals are unchanged. Grants were checked per role, and an empty database runs both migrations twice as a no-op. GUARDS: src/test/messageGateCurrentParty.test.ts (effective definitions; 7 @mutate lines on the newest migration) and src/lib/offJobGate.test.tsx (notice render, no role words, send-path and ChatView wiring on comment-blanked source; 4 @mutate lines). To verify on prod after db-deploy: pg_get_functiondef of public.can_message_in_job(uuid,uuid) contains `j.direct_offer_status = 'pending'` and `a.status IN ('pending', 'accepted')`; that of public.can_send_message_to_in_job(uuid,uuid) contains `AND NOT public.is_off_job(_job_id, _receiver)`; proacl: can_message_in_job and is_off_job {postgres, service_role}, can_send_message_to_in_job and get_off_job_thread_state {postgres, authenticated, service_role}. Needs an lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass before landing.

- [~] **Q407** (OWNER DECISIONS, group + recurring jobs, owner 2026-09-25, answered in the session; these settle Q394, Q395 and the recurring lane's owner questions): GROUP: (1) a crew has NO lead: every hired member is equal in access (address, messaging the poster), pay share, cancellation-fee share and review; (2) a late poster cancellation's fee is split EVENLY across the hired crew; (3) one review PER Helpr, each counting toward that Helpr's own score and tier. RECURRING: (4) the poster chooses AT POSTING TIME between 'one person for every visit' and 'OK to split the days'; (5) when split, the first hired Helpr picks the visit dates they want, the poster then offers the remaining dates to the next Helpr they choose, and so on until every date is covered; a date nobody has picked stays offerable while the poster keeps offering it, and if it is still unfilled when it arrives it is not charged; (6) a Helpr giving up one of their dates (or ending their part of a series) gets a reliability strike ONLY within 24 hours of that visit, same as a late single-job cancel, and the date goes back to the poster to offer again; (7) a poster with a running series CANNOT delete their account until the series is ended; (8) after hire, a one-time job's date/time can change only as a request the Helpr accepts; (9) banning an account ends every series it posts or works on (future visits cancelled and not charged, the other party told). Build: group redesign on top of the reviewed roster-departure rework (group-fix 212b6db40); recurring split-days on top of recurring-fix. Each ships with a class guard and a money/authz review. **GROUP (1)-(3) BUILT 2026-09-25 on branch group-fix, NOT deployed, NOT pushed (migration 20260925154606_group_crew_has_no_lead + edge + client):** jobs.helper_id is NULL on every group job, enforced in every context by trg_group_job_has_no_lead (backfill keeps any former lead on the roster); helpers_needed / is_group_job locked once funded or hired (money review HIGH-1); each member's share frozen in cents at hire (group_job_helpers.slot_no / share_cents, largest remainder: $100/3 = 3334+3333+3333, MEDIUM-3) and paid from it by process-scheduled-payouts; every hire needs a funded job; proof photos: every member uploads to and reads the job folder, written to their own roster row through rpc_group_member_set_proof (PhotoProof crew path, HelperPhotoAsk reads the member's own slot); completion/cancel/payout notices go to every member; cancel_escrow re-reads the roster after its claim and pages on a stuck claim; an under-filled crew completes when every HIRED member is done and the unfilled shares are refunded to the poster (rule crew_completes_when_hired_done(), default true, owner to confirm, MEDIUM-4); tips: manual tip names a roster member (ReviewForm's 5-star prompt tips the reviewed member), auto-tip splits the poster's auto-tip across the crew (one claim per member); money-reconciliation grades a crew member by member (crew_transfers_exceed_escrow, crew_member_unpaid_on_released_job, per-member tier). FOUND AND FIXED on the way: accept_group_application's status CASE assigned text to the job_status enum, so every crew hire since 20260804122000 raised (pglite R0; not checked live). GUARDS: src/test/groupCrewNoLead.test.ts (class guard: the invariant, an exact two-way inventory of 14 helper_id-keyed functions that grant nothing on a crew, the hire, owner rules SQL==TS, fee ledger, reviews, proof photos, tips, cancel_escrow; 16 @mutate, all red), src/test/crewShareAllocation.test.ts (every budget $0-$3,000 x N=1..8 sums exactly; 2 @mutate), src/test/pglite/groupCrewNoLead.pglite.mjs (R0-R5 red on the before state, A1-A38 green, applied 3x), src/test/edge/void-cancelled-payments-crew.test.ts, process-scheduled-payouts-crew.test.ts, money-reconciliation-crew.test.ts, create-payment.test.ts (crew cancel_escrow + crew tip). OPEN: two owner rules to confirm (crew_fee_pays_unconfirmed, crew_completes_when_hired_done); the follow-ups in Q709 and Q710; screenshots before/after of the poster's completed crew card (Review chip, no Tip chip), the Cancel dialog on a crew job (fee lines), the post-form crew split line, and a crew member's On-site/Working card (Before/After Photo chips) — group jobs are behind GROUP_JOBS_ENABLED=false, so none is reachable in prod until the flag flips; NEEDS a REVIEW-ONLY lh-money-escrow + lh-authz-rls pass on 19d40e3ca..HEAD. To verify on prod after db-deploy: see the prod queries in the lane report (pg_get_functiondef/proacl of the restated functions, pg_trigger for trg_group_job_has_no_lead / trg_freeze_crew_member_share, pg_constraint reviews_one_per_reviewee_per_job, pg_indexes tips_one_auto_per_job_member, `SELECT count(*) FROM jobs WHERE is_group_job AND helper_id IS NOT NULL` = 0). ADDENDUM (owner, 2026-09-25, answered in the session): (10) on a SPLIT series a permanent ban of ONE Helpr hands back only that Helpr's dates to the poster to offer again (the other Helprs keep theirs; a handed-back date still unfilled when it arrives is not charged); a permanent ban of the POSTER still ends the whole series; a TEMPORARY ban pauses the series (confirmed); (11) a Helpr cancelling a ONE-TIME job gets a reliability strike ONLY within 24h of the start, the same rule as series visits; (12) the platform NEVER absorbs a fee: a refund of an already-charged visit (unfilled, ban-ended) returns the amount paid minus Stripe's processing fee, never more than the platform received net; (13) group jobs, CORRECTED by the owner the same day: a late-cancel fee is shared by every crew member once the poster has OFFERED them the job (hired), whether or not they confirmed (crew_fee_pays_unconfirmed() = true, as built); an under-filled crew completes and the unfilled shares are refunded (confirmed). The owner wants a detailed review later of 'once offered vs once confirmed' across ALL fees (Q706); (14) messaging closes BOTH ways once someone is off a job (rejected applicant, removed crew member, declined/expired offeree): neither side can start new messages on that job, old ones stay readable; (15) confirmed as built: on a split series the first hired Helpr has visit one; never-picked dates are offered by the poster, only given-up dates are open to the series' other Helprs to claim. **Crew disputes, reminders and counts under these rules: Q728 (2026-09-27).** RECURRING (4)-(12), the pick-up addendum and (15): landing on main 2026-09-27 (see Q733 for commits, guards and live checks), the remaining owner questions in Q415, deferred work in Q734 and Q417. RECURRING (4)-(12),(15) LIVE on prod 2026-09-27 (Q733 has the SHAs and live checks); only the Q415 owner questions and Q734/Q417 remain.

REVIEWED (lh-money-escrow REVIEW-ONLY, 2026-09-25) and every finding fixed: M1 a teardown sweep (SWEEP_PHASE=teardown, the `if: always()` step) now fails on a failed helper mint, an unreadable HELPER_ACCESS_TOKEN fails every sweep, and a hired+funded row past 48h that a both-seats test-mode teardown could not settle fails it (a pre-sweep warns, so the suite still runs); M2 e2e-abuse-notifications' suites job holds `prod-lifecycle-shared-accounts` (its dispatch now splits like e2e-journeys; rule-4 exemption removed from prodWorkflowSpacing); M3 `[E2E HOLD]` in a title makes the sweep and settleJobForward leave a row alone (documented in scripts/e2e/README.md and e2e/two-role-lifecycle.spec.ts), and the helper-seat timeout inventory counts workflow-level env and session secrets; L1 the create-payment window is stamped in a `finally`; L2 settling forward needs repo variable E2E_STRIPE_MODE=test (stripe-sandbox-on.sh sets it after the test key, stripe-sandbox-off.sh sets live BEFORE the live key and refuses to continue otherwise) AND a cs_test_ Checkout Session on the row; L3 the listing is `customer_id=eq.<poster>&is_seed=is.true` and a zero-row reopen PATCH is a failure; L4 CompletionPrompts' review insert is `.select("id")` + unwrapMutation, and only a proven row (or 23505) marks the cache Reviewed; L5 guard extended. GUARDS: src/test/sweeperFailsClosed.test.ts (runs the real sweeper against a local PostgREST stand-in and sweep-both-seats.sh beside stub mints; 7 @mutate, all red), src/test/sweeperHoldsBothSeats.test.ts (+6 @mutate: job-level lock per sweeping job, SWEEP_PHASE per step, E2E_STRIPE_MODE per step, sandbox-off ordering), src/test/reviewWritesUpdateActivityCache.test.ts (+4), src/test/journeyTeardownSettlesForward.test.ts (+2), src/test/sweepSparesCreatePaymentWindow.test.ts (+1). PENDING (lead/owner): the repo variable E2E_STRIPE_MODE does not exist yet (not set from this container, and whether prod is in test mode right now was not measured here: health-check's stripe_mode is admin-gated and the Supabase MCP was down). Until someone confirms test mode and runs `gh variable set E2E_STRIPE_MODE --body test`, every CI sweep DEFERS hired+funded rows with a warning instead of settling them, so #1719's escrow rows will not clear. RE-REVIEW (sonnet, 2026-09-25) of 5a22b3e10 confirmed M1, M2, L1, L3, L4/L5 and found two gaps, both fixed: (a) the two-role fixture is now held BY ID: every sweep step gets secret PLAYWRIGHT_LIFECYCLE_JOB_ID, and heldReason() in scripts/e2e/settleForward.mjs makes the sweeper and settleRefusalReason leave that id alone whatever its live title says (unset holds nothing extra); (b) a hired+funded row with NULL stripe_session_id (create-payment's gift-card path) is listed as held with a ::warning naming it and never counted in the 48h teardown failure; only stale rows with a cs_test_ session count, and a cs_live_ row is refused and warned about. GUARDS: sweeperFailsClosed (+3 tests, +2 @mutate), journeyTeardownSettlesForward (+2 tests, +2 @mutate), sweeperHoldsBothSeats (secret on every sweep step, +1 @mutate).

(2) Same with Sign in with Apple choosing "Share My Email" -> same account. (3) Sign in with Apple choosing "Hide My Email" -> a NEW account on the Complete your profile form (expected; see Q447). (4) Sign up with email+password and do NOT click the verify link; then Continue with Google on that address -> you are signed in to that account, and the password you set no longer works (Forgot Password restores it; see Q447). - [ ] **Q447** (OWNER DECISION, OA-018 follow-ups, 2026-09-25, from GoTrue's source, not reproduced on a device): (a) PRE-ACCOUNT DATA, **MEDIUM** (rated by the lh-authz-rls review of #1806, 2026-09-26): when an UNverified email account is linked (Q446 step 4), GoTrue removes its password and email identity to stop a pre-account takeover, but OUR profile row (name, phone, DOB, photo, city, bio, marketing consent, legal acceptances, referral) is kept as whoever typed it at signup. Wider than first listed (code read 2026-09-26): complete-signup's unauthenticated path (supabase/functions/complete-signup/index.ts ~200-269: account under 30 min old, never signed in, empty bio) also takes the avatar, license and insurance uploads, writes legal_acceptances and records the referral, and because it fills every ProtectedRoute gate field the victim never passes /complete-profile to see it; the attacker's phone becomes the contact shown to counterparties. GoTrue leaves the attacker no session, refresh token, MFA factor or phone identity (token.go refuses the password grant to an unconfirmed email; signup refuses email+phone together), and cleanup-abandoned-accounts deletes unconfirmed accounts after 30 days. Referral attribution should also require a confirmed email. If an attacker signed up first with a victim's address, the victim's Google sign-in lands in an account whose profile the attacker wrote. Options: keep (today), or clear the signup-entered profile fields when email_confirmed_at is first set by a provider link (no email identity left), sending the person back through /complete-profile. Clearing also costs a legitimate user who signed up and then used Google instead of the email link. (b) APPLE HIDE-MY-EMAIL makes a second account for someone who already has one; /complete-profile could say "Already have a Helpr account under another email? Sign out and log in with it" when the address is @privaterelay.appleid.com. Guard to name when done: a case in scripts/sql/identity-linking-scenarios.sql for (a); a /complete-profile test for (b).

- [ ] **Q572** (LOW, found working Q349, 2026-09-26, measured once): listing a Storage bucket as anon fails with an ERROR instead of a clean empty/denied answer. `POST /storage/v1/object/list/avatars` with the publishable key returned 403 `{"code":"AccessDenied","message":"permission denied for table jobs"}`, so a storage.objects SELECT policy reads public.jobs, which anon has no grant on. The refusal itself is correct (anon should not list avatars); the problem is that it comes from a permission error inside a policy rather than the policy's own condition, so any anon storage read that reaches that policy errors. Not read: pg_policies for storage.objects (no service role here). To close: find the policy with `select policyname, qual from pg_policies where schemaname='storage' and tablename='objects' and qual ilike '%jobs%'`, and scope it `TO authenticated` (or guard the jobs read) so anon gets a plain deny; add it to the anon-grant class guard.

- [~] **Q511** (lh-authz-rls review of Q335, 2026-09-26; pre-existing, not introduced by Q335): a Restore on one device was undone by another — unarchiveConversation deleted the server row, but a second device's local mirror still held the key and its next loadArchives merge-up pushed it back, hiding the thread everywhere. Fixed 2026-09-26 (owner: "fix Q511 too", branch cloud/open-messaging-deleted): archivedConversations keeps a per-user PENDING set (safeStorage `helpr_archived_conversations_pending_<uid>`) of archives this device made and never saw confirmed; archiveConversation marks pending and clears on a confirmed write (or rollback), unarchiveConversation clears it, and the merge-up (pure `planMergeUp`) pushes only pending keys and DROPS a local-only key the server confirmed once and no longer has. Upgrade note: a device's first load after this ships has no pending set, so any archive it made offline and never synced is dropped once (the thread reappears and can be archived again) — chosen over re-pushing, which is the bug. Guard: src/test/mirrorMergeUpPushesOnlyPending.test.ts (class = every src/lib module with a safeStorage mirror and an .upsert merge-up, derived from source; KNOWN_PUSHES_ALL exact both ways; 4 @mutate lines, each shown red). Left: live two-device check (restore on device B, reload device A, thread stays in the inbox) — not possible from this session (no test-account credentials).

- [ ] **Q550** (found by Q326, 2026-09-26): a11y-webkit-prod.yml's sweep job signs in as the shared test accounts but does not hold the `prod-lifecycle-shared-accounts` job lock. Its two-engine matrix (chromium + webkit side by side for the diff) is why: one lock would serialise the engines, and GitHub's one-pending-per-group rule could cancel the queued engine. Its workflow group is prod-load on every trigger, so it never overlaps a scheduled suite or a press run, but a DISPATCHED split suite (journeys, prod-audit, slow-network, loading-states, privacy) can still overlap it. Fix: run both engines inside one job that holds the lock, as press-every-control now does (scripts/audit/press-wave.sh), then take its dispatch out of prod-load and remove it from DISPATCH_SHARES_GROUP. Guard to update: src/test/sharedAccountJobsHoldOneLock.test.ts (delete its NOT_LOCKED entry; the test fails until you do).
- [ ] **Q551** (found by Q326, 2026-09-26): vacuity.yml's vacuity job holds shared-account credentials without the account lock. It runs on every push, and in the lock each push's guard proof would queue behind a 5-hour press run and be cancelled by the next push. It signs in only when a registered e2e guard's files changed, but then it drives the shared accounts beside whatever else is running. Fix options: move the credentialed (e2e) registrations into a separate job that holds the lock and runs only when such a registration is selected; or make those registrations use a dedicated seed account no other suite drives. - [ ] **Q721 OWNER DECISION: stale-deploy WebKit red (#1863) vs the Q178 first-paint budget** (measured 2026-09-26, local `vite preview` + `happy-path-webkit`). The 12 red tests in stale-deploy.spec.ts ("warm, guard armed … Try Again recovers" ×10, boot "stale HTML" ×2) all fail the same way: when a chunk fetch fails, WebKit caches the failure, and the recovery reload (`hardReloadBypassCache`, `_v=` URL) re-imports the same chunk URL from that cache without touching the network, so the page stays blank. The failure comes from Q178's `modulePreload.resolveDependencies` returning the deps list, which includes the chunk itself. Four variants measured:
  - `() => []`: **34/34 WebKit pass**, but `critical-path.mjs --check` gives 11 failures. The page chunk moves from round 2 to round 3, and readiness takes 7-8 rounds instead of 2, on /, /browse, /login, /signup and /posts.
  - `deps.filter(d => d !== filename)`: 10/10 fail.
  - Removing the `<link rel=modulepreload>` tags before the reload: 10/10 fail.
  - Keeping deps only when the importer is the entry or main chunk: 12 fail AND the perf budget still fails.
  
  In a real stale deploy the new HTML carries new hashes, so this bites only on a transient chunk failure (a network blip). **Choose:** (a) keep fast first paint and accept that Try Again can't recover from a transient chunk failure in Safari/iOS; the test would then expect a manual relaunch, which needs an owner decision because it changes what the test proves. (b) Recovery first: `resolveDependencies: () => []` and re-baseline the Q178 budgets (a slower first paint on phones). (c) Research a third way, e.g. a `Clear-Site-Data: "cache"` response on `_v=` reloads. Its WebKit support has not been verified.

- [~] **Q740** (nightly-red #1794 a11y-webkit-prod + #1654 loading-states-refresh, 2026-09-27): the pacer's single learned reserve. Capped at half the per-worker share (100 of 200), a list page's burst landed on a part-filled minute: a11y-prod-webkit busiest minute 439/400 (run 36263866330). Uncapped (0d0010181), one burst over the 200 share made EVERY gate wait for an empty minute, one page per minute: both a11y legs cancelled at the 60-min job timeout at test ~330/368 (36271541309) and loading-states cancelled the same way (36272891811). FIX: e2e/requestMeter.mjs learns the burst PER PAGE (gate key = path): a known page reserves its own burst, an unknown one min(half share, largest burst); samples carry `topBursts`, printed by the budget step. Guard src/test/requestMeterPacing.test.ts (8/8 mutations killed). 36218568850 (cancelled) never started a job: cancelled in the queue. OPEN until a dispatched run of each workflow completes green (busiest minute <= 400, inside 60 min, loading-states baseline re-prove passes). RE-RUN a11y-webkit-prod 36294688001 (main 0d2d66ebe, 2026-09-27): 368 passed, 0 skipped on both legs (skips cleared); chromium 38.3 min, busiest minute 371/400 PASS; webkit 40.5 min (timeouts cleared), busiest minute 437/400 FAIL. The breach is ONE page burst, not pacing: `/jobs/6e1a1c36-3d62-4b7d-8eea-fdbd233d52c2` (job-detail-completed) sent 330 requests on webkit (194 chromium) between two gates, more than the whole 200 per-worker share, so no gate can bound it. It is Q430's root cause: the poster's own /jobs/:id redirects to /posts?highlight=, the Done bucket renders all 69 completed cards and auto-expands every untipped one (JobTracking query + channel + avatar each). The test poster's residue grows every lifecycle run (160 cancelled, 69 completed, 16 in_progress). Secondary: the a11y job-detail-* tests never render job detail, they audit My Posts (same redirect). #1794 stays red until Q430 is fixed (owner UX call). The run's overall 'cancelled' is its fixtures job, bumped from prod-lifecycle-shared-accounts by press 36294859009; 36218568850 and loading-states 36294688957 were cancelled the same way (a newer pending request in the group cancels the older one). AVATAR CLASS (2026-09-27): the run's top repeated GET, `/storage/v1/object/public/avatars/:id/avatar.jpg` x719, was an APP defect, not the meter. UserAvatar set `src` before `crossOrigin` (React applies props in order), so WebKit/WKWebView started a no-cors load, then a second CORS load, and shared neither with the next <img> of the same URL (cache headers are fine: GET max-age=3600). Measured in Playwright WebKit, 40 <img> of one URL: src-first 41 requests, crossOrigin-first 1 (Chromium 1 either way). On a local build against prod /posts?filter=done, one Helpr avatar went 101 -> 1 request, all storage requests 95 -> 42 (evidence ~/.lh-shots/avatar-meter/). FIX: src/components/UserAvatar.tsx sets crossOrigin/loading before src. Guard src/test/imgCrossOriginBeforeSrc.test.tsx (every JSX <img> in src/, floor >30; plus rendered attribute order): red on origin/main and under its @mutate, green on the fix. The Q430 page burst above remains; re-run dispatched to measure what is left.

The helper branch computes `budget/N × (1 − helper_fee_percent)`; the canonical helperTakeHomeDollars (src/lib/helperEarnings.ts) is `budget/N − helperPlatformFeeDollars (stamped amount first on a released row) + netUrgentFeeDollars(urgent_fee)/N`, so an urgent job, or one whose stamped fee differs from the percent, shows the admin a different take-home than the helper's own Earnings screen. Done when calcEarning's helper branch calls helperTakeHomeDollars, with a class check that every helper-earnings figure in src/ goes through helperEarnings.ts, shown red on the current JobsTab.

Same class (re-review of the fix, 2026-09-27): a card declined INSIDE a still-open Checkout Session marks the job failed and sends "the job isn't posted... load your draft", yet a retry in that session succeeds (failed -> escrow) and the job goes live; tell the poster from checkout.session.expired instead, or soften the copy. DONE 2026-09-27: the not-posted notice moved to checkout.session.expired (only for a job still failed), the draft records its checkout job and Entry hides Load Draft once paid and warns while a session is open (from the first render); guard `src/test/declineNoticeWaitsForSessionExpiry.test.ts`, red on 116fd86f8. lh-money-escrow: PASS.
